/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/discussion.tex:12:At the 1v2 boundary, $\auc$ drops by approximately 30 percentage points ($\auc$ range: 0.6112--0.7039 across all eight model$\times$decoding conditions; Table~\ref{tab:kstar_ci}, Figure~\ref{fig:auc_decay}; see also Figure~\ref{fig:s1_auc_decay_ci} in the Appendix), indicating that the majority of the discriminative signal is consumed in the first generation step.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/discussion.tex:13:By the 2v3 boundary, $\auc$ falls to 0.5496--0.5917 across all eight model$\times$decoding conditions (upper bound from nucleus $p{=}0.9$ decoding; Figure~\ref{fig:auc_decay})---below the operational threshold $\theta = 0.60$---and deeper pairs converge toward chance.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/discussion.tex:20:Crucially, this degradation ordering is consistent across model families (Spearman $\rho \geq 0.85$ for all continuation-mode pairs; Table~\ref{tab:top_features}), suggesting that the two-step information loss is driven primarily by the autoregressive generation mechanism rather than by model-specific biases at this scale.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/discussion.tex:40:This robustness suggests that, at least at the $\leq$1.5B scale, $\Kempirical$ is governed primarily by the information bottleneck of the generation process rather than by surface-level properties of the sampling algorithm.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/discussion.tex:46:Classifiers trained on Pythia-1.4B and evaluated on OLMo-1B retain 90--92\% of within-model accuracy, and the broader continuation-mode transfer matrix shows 80--92\% retention across all model pairs (Table~\ref{tab:transfer_matrix}; see Table~\ref{tab:s1_transfer} for the full matrix).
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/discussion.tex:47:At the 0v1 boundary, cross-model $\auc$ exceeds 0.96 across all tested model pairs, confirming that binary contamination detection is fully transferable across the tested generators.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/discussion.tex:65:This dissociation reveals an important distinction: the \emph{existence} of the depth boundary is robust, but the \emph{optimal feature subset} for detecting it depends on the sampling strategy.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/discussion.tex:69:The cross-model feature ordering (Section~\ref{sec:feature_degradation}) provides a complementary perspective: among continuation-mode models, single-feature $\auc$ rankings are highly consistent (Spearman $\rho \geq 0.85$), with surprisal-variance features---\texttt{surp\_d1\_std}, \texttt{surp\_std}, \texttt{surp\_d2\_std}---dominating across all three families.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/discussion.tex:75:Our experiments establish $\Kempirical = 2$ at the $\leq$1.5B parameter scale, providing the first empirical measurement of the generational depth boundary for language models.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/discussion.tex:77:All 15 features are computed from surface-level token statistics (surprisal moments, vocabulary diversity, frequency tail measures) without access to model internals, and the cross-model transfer results (80--92\% retention across architecturally distinct model families; Section~\ref{sec:transfer}) already demonstrate that the methodology generalizes across different architectures and training corpora at the tested scale.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/discussion.tex:131:The boundary is robust within a practical range but not invariant to threshold choice; our conclusions are conditioned on $\theta = 0.60$.

```text
25	\title{Generational Depth Estimation of Recursive Synthetic Text:\\Measuring the Discrimination Boundary}
26	
27	\author{Anonymous}
28	
29	\begin{document}
30	\maketitle
31	
32	% === Abstract ===
33	\begin{abstract}
34	Web-scale text corpora are increasingly contaminated by recursive synthetic content---text recursively generated across multiple generations. Information degrades monotonically per the Data Processing Inequality with each generation, yet no method estimates a document's generational depth. We introduce the first systematic framework for generational depth estimation. Our approach extracts 15 statistical features and classifies depth via Ordinal Binary Decomposition (OBD) with LightGBM, preserving ordinal structure. We empirically measure the discrimination boundary $\Kempirical$, the maximum depth at which adjacent generations remain statistically distinguishable. Across 4 model-mode configurations and 4 decoding strategies (8 conditions total) at the $\leq$1.5B parameter scale, we find $\Kempirical=2$ is consistent: bootstrap 95\% CI lower bounds for 1-vs-2 across the four model-mode configurations all exceed $\theta=0.60$ while all 2-vs-3 CI upper bounds fall below it; decoding strategies likewise yield $\auc$ above $\theta$ at 1-vs-2 and below at 2-vs-3. Cross-model transfer shows classifiers retain 80--92\% accuracy across continuation-mode generators, with binary (0-vs-1) $\auc$ exceeding 0.96, indicating depth features are largely text-intrinsic. Preliminary neural probing on one dataset yields $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}}=3$, suggesting our 15-feature $\Kempirical=2$ is a representation-dependent lower bound. Frontier LLMs achieve ${\sim}88\%$ binary detection but only ${\sim}33\%$ five-class depth accuracy---below our OBD classifier's 38.6\%---confirming depth estimation as a non-trivial task requiring specialized features. Our findings motivate a three-tier taxonomy (human / generation-1 / generation-2+) for depth-aware data curation; we investigate whether this coarse ordinal signal enables graduated filtering that outperforms binary approaches discarding all synthetic content indiscriminately.
35	\end{abstract}
36

1	\section{Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:conclusion}
3	
4	% === Contribution 1: K*=2 consistent boundary ===
5	% Sources: exp_007 (bootstrap CI + permutation), exp_001/exp_002/exp_017/mvp_pilot (model-mode), exp_003 (decoding ablation)
6	We presented the first systematic measurement of the generational depth
7	discrimination boundary $\Kempirical$ for recursive synthetic text.
8	Across three model architectures in four model-mode configurations
9	(Pythia-1.4B and OLMo-1B in continuation mode; Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct in both
10	continuation and rewrite modes) and 4 decoding strategies---totaling 8
11	conditions---we establish $\Kempirical = 2$ as a consistent boundary:
12	classifiers reliably distinguish adjacent generations up to depth~2, but all
13	deeper pairs fall below the operational threshold $\theta = 0.60$.
14	For the four model-mode configurations, bootstrap 95\% confidence intervals
15	and permutation tests confirm statistical significance at the 1v2 boundary
16	($p \leq 0.002$ for all four); the most marginal case, Qwen-continuation,
17	yields a CI lower bound of 0.6015, exceeding $\theta$ by only 0.15\,pp.
18	All four decoding configurations (greedy, nucleus, $T{=}0.70$, $T{=}1.2$)
19	likewise yield 1v2 point-estimate $\auc$ above $\theta$.
20	The 0v1 boundary is near-perfectly separable ($\auc > 0.99$), followed by a
21	sharp ${\sim}$30\,pp drop at 1v2 ($\auc$: 0.6112--0.7039 across all
22	conditions), while all 2v3 pairs fall below~$\theta$.
23	Recursive generation thus acts as a severely lossy channel whose discriminable
24	statistical fingerprint is exhausted within two steps.
25	
26	% === Contribution 2: Ordinal depth estimation framework ===
27	% Sources: exp_010 (OBD vs baselines), exp_013 (cross-model transfer), exp_005 (feature degradation), exp_011 (LLM zero-shot)
28	To operationalize depth estimation, we introduced an ordinal binary
29	decomposition (OBD) framework pairing 15 hand-crafted statistical features
30	with LightGBM classifiers.
31	Cross-model transfer experiments demonstrate 80--92\% accuracy retention
32	across continuation-mode model pairs, with
33	Pythia$\leftrightarrow$OLMo achieving 90--92\% and 0v1 transfer $\auc$
34	exceeding 0.96 across all tested model pairs---indicating that depth signals are largely
35	text-intrinsic rather than generator-specific.
36	Feature degradation orderings are consistent across architectures
37	(Spearman $\rho \geq 0.85$), with surprisal-variance features dominating
38	across all tested families, suggesting that the two-step information loss is driven primarily by the
39	autoregressive mechanism at this scale.
40	As a reference point, frontier LLMs (GPT-5.5, Claude Opus~4.6) achieve
41	${\sim}$88\% binary human-vs.-synthetic accuracy but only ${\sim}$33\% on
42	5-class depth estimation, below OBD-LightGBM's 38.6\%---confirming that
43	fine-grained depth discrimination requires specialized statistical features
44	rather than general language understanding.
45	A preliminary neural probing experiment suggests that this operational bound is not tight: a frozen Qwen2.5-1.5B encoder paired with an MLP classifier achieves $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}} = 3$ on the Qwen-continuation dataset (2v3 $\auc = 0.6195$ versus 0.5609 with hand-crafted features), indicating that learned representations recover depth signal beyond the 15-dimensional feature set.
46	This result, pending cross-model validation, provides initial evidence that $\Ktheory \geq 3$.
47	
48	% === Contribution 3: Graduated filtering (C3) ===
49	% Source: exp_006 (RUNNING — results pending)
50	The $\Kempirical = 2$ boundary implies a natural trichotomy---human (depth~0), first-generation synthetic (depth~1), and deeper synthetic (depth~$\geq 2$)---that can be exploited for depth-aware data curation.
51	% TODO: exp-006 graduated filtering 具体结果填充。
52	% 预留内容：graduated filtering 利用三级分类法在 MMLU/HellaSwag 上的表现，
53	% 与 binary filtering 和 no-filter baseline 的对比。
54	
55	% === Limitations + Future Work ===
56	Our findings are bounded by the scale of models tested ($\leq$1.5B parameters), restriction to English C4 text, and the use of prompt-chain rather than retrain-chain protocols; $\Kempirical$ depends on the threshold $\theta$ and shifts with generation mode (continuation-mode configurations drop to $\Kempirical{=}1$ at $\theta{=}0.65$, while rewrite mode retains $\Kempirical{=}2$; Section~\ref{sec:limitations}); the 15-dimensional feature set yields $\Kempirical$ as an operational lower bound, and $\Ktheory$ may be strictly higher.
57	Several directions merit investigation: scaling to larger models (7B+) to test whether increased generation fidelity shifts the boundary; extending to multilingual and multi-domain corpora; establishing the relationship between prompt-chain and retrain-chain $\Kstar$ values, which would connect our measurements to the model collapse setting; incorporating few-shot or chain-of-thought prompting for LLM-based depth estimation; and extending the neural probe approach---which has yielded preliminary evidence of $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}} = 3$ on one dataset---through cross-model validation (Pythia, OLMo), bootstrap confidence intervals, multi-layer probing beyond the last hidden state, and comparison with fine-tuned (non-frozen) encoders to further narrow the gap between $\Kempirical$ and $\Ktheory$.
58	
```

1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	We evaluate generational depth estimation on four model configurations:
5	Pythia-1.4B and OLMo-1B in continuation mode, and Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct in both rewrite and continuation modes.
6	All experiments use the OBD-LightGBM classifier with the 15-dimensional feature set described in Section~\ref{sec:method}.
7	Each configuration uses a balanced corpus of 5{,}000 texts per depth level (generations 0--5).
8	We organize results around four questions:
9	(1)~Does $\Kempirical = 2$ hold across configurations?
10	(2)~Do depth classifiers transfer across models?
11	(3)~Is the feature degradation ordering model-independent?
12	(4)~How does generation mode affect detectability?
13	
14	%% ====================================================================
15	\subsection{$\Kempirical = 2$ Across All Configurations}
16	\label{sec:kstar_boundary}
17	
18	Table~\ref{tab:kstar_ci} reports pairwise $\auc$ for adjacent depth pairs
19	across all four configurations, with bootstrap 95\% confidence intervals
20	(1{,}000 resamples) and permutation-test $p$-values for the critical 1v2
21	and 2v3 boundaries.
22	
23	\begin{table}[t]
24	\centering
25	\small
26	\caption{Pairwise $\auc$ for adjacent depth pairs.  Bootstrap 95\% CIs

28	2v3).  $\Kempirical = 2$ requires 1v2 $\text{CI}_\text{low} > \theta$
29	and 2v3 $\text{CI}_\text{high} < \theta$ with $\theta = 0.60$.
30	All 0v1 permutation $p \leq 0.002$ (Qwen-continuation 0v1: CI [0.9963, 0.9978], $p = 0.002$).}
31	\label{tab:kstar_ci}
32	\begin{tabular}{lcccc}
33	\toprule
34	 & Qwen-rw & Pythia & OLMo & Qwen-cont \\
35	\midrule
36	0v1 & 0.9956 & 0.9932 & 0.9970 & 0.9971 \\[4pt]
37	1v2 & 0.6955 & 0.6539 & 0.6145 & 0.6112 \\

40	  & {\footnotesize [.6454,\,.6630]}
41	  & {\footnotesize [.6045,\,.6247]}
42	  & {\footnotesize [.6015,\,.6213]} \\
43	{\footnotesize $p$}
44	  & {\footnotesize .002}
45	  & {\footnotesize .002}
46	  & {\footnotesize .002}
47	  & {\footnotesize .002} \\[4pt]

51	  & {\footnotesize [.5417,\,.5628]}
52	  & {\footnotesize [.5399,\,.5596]}
53	  & {\footnotesize [.5511,\,.5712]} \\

64	
65	The pattern is consistent across all four configurations:
66	0v1 discrimination is near-perfect ($\auc > 0.99$),
67	followed by a sharp drop at 1v2 ($\auc$ range: 0.6112--0.6955),
68	with 2v3 falling below the decision threshold $\theta = 0.60$
69	($\auc$ range: 0.5496--0.5893).
70	All subsequent pairs (3v4, 4v5) converge toward chance.
71	Figure~\ref{fig:auc_decay} visualizes this decay.

All configurations show a sharp drop between 0v1 and 1v2, with 2v3 and deeper pairs falling below the decision threshold $\theta = 0.60$ (horizontal dashed line).  Error bars show bootstrap 95\% CIs for model-mode configurations; decoding strategy curves omit CIs as they were computed on a separate experimental setup.}
77	\label{fig:auc_decay}
78	\end{figure}
79	
80	For the $\Kempirical$ determination, all four 1v2 CI lower bounds exceed
81	$\theta$: Qwen-rewrite ($\text{CI}_\text{low} = 0.6859$), Pythia (0.6454),
82	OLMo (0.6045), and Qwen-continuation (0.6015).
83	Conversely, all four 2v3 CI upper bounds fall below $\theta$:
84	Qwen-rewrite ($\text{CI}_\text{high} = 0.5991$), Qwen-continuation (0.5712),
85	Pythia (0.5628), and OLMo (0.5596).
86	Permutation tests confirm $p = 0.002$ for all eight boundary comparisons.
87	Together these establish $\Kempirical = 2$ as a consistent discrimination boundary across all tested configurations at the $\leq$1.5B scale.
88	
89	The most marginal case is Qwen-continuation, where the 1v2 CI lower bound
90	(0.6015) exceeds $\theta$ by only 0.0015.  This narrow margin
91	indicates that $\Kempirical = 2$ is robust but not trivially so---continuation-mode
92	generation produces weaker depth artifacts than rewrite mode, pushing the
93	boundary closer to the detection threshold.
94	
95	The absolute 1v2 $\auc$ varies across models:
96	Qwen-rewrite (0.6955) $>$ Pythia (0.6539) $>$ OLMo (0.6145) $\approx$ Qwen-continuation (0.6112).
97	Notably, the spread due to generation mode within a single model
98	(Qwen-rewrite vs.\ Qwen-continuation: $\Delta = 8.4$\,pp) exceeds the spread
99	across models in the same mode
100	(Pythia vs.\ OLMo: $\Delta = 3.9$\,pp),

Error bars show bootstrap 95\\% CIs for model-mode configurations; decoding strategy curves omit CIs as they were computed on a separate experimental setup.}\n\\label{fig:auc_decay}\n\\end{figure}\n\nFor the $\\Kempirical$ determination, all four 1v2 CI lower bounds exceed\n$\\theta$: Qwen-rewrite ($\\text{CI}_\\text{low} = 0.6859$), Pythia (0.6454),\nOLMo (0.6045), and Qwen-continuation (0.6015).\nConversely, all four 2v3 CI upper bounds fall below $\\theta$:\nQwen-rewrite ($\\text{CI}_\\text{high} = 0.5991$), Qwen-continuation (0.5712),\nPythia (0.5628), and OLMo (0.5596).\nPermutation tests confirm $p = 0.002$ for all eight boundary comparisons.\nTogether these establish $\\Kempirical = 2$ as a consistent discrimination boundary across all tested configurations at the $\\leq$1.5B scale.\n\nThe most marginal case is Qwen-continuation, where the 1v2 CI lower bound\n(0.6015) exceeds $\\theta$ by only 0.0015.  This narrow margin\nindicates that $\\Kempirical = 2$ is robust but not trivially so---continuation-mode\ngeneration produces weaker depth artifacts than rewrite mode, pushing the\nboundary closer to the detection threshold.\n\nThe absolute 1v2 $\\auc$ varies across models:\nQwen-rewrite (0.6955) $>$ Pythia (0.6539) $>$ OLMo (0.6145) $\\approx$ Qwen-continuation (0.6112).\nNotably, the spread due to generation mode within a single model\n(Qwen-rewrite vs.\\ Qwen-continuation: $\\Delta = 8.4$\\,pp) exceeds the spread\nacross models in the same mode\n(Pythia vs.\\ OLMo: $\\Delta = 3.9$\\,pp),",
      "numLines": 100,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 342
    }
  },

的行为，发现 K*=2 在所有 8 个测试条件下保持不变，展现出跨模型架构、跨解码策略、跨生成模式的 universality。\\n\\n**子组件：**\\n\\n**(a) K*=2 universality across architectures, generation modes, and decoding strategies**\\n> **4/4 model-mode configurations 与 4/4 decoding strategies 全部 K*=2：**\\n>\\n> *Model × mode (exp-007):*\\n> | Model | AUC 1v2 | 95% CI | CI lower > 0.60? |\\n> |-------|---------|--------|-----------------|\\n> | 1v2 range [0.6866, 0.7039]，K* 完全不变\\n> - K*=2 在所有 8 个测试条件下保持不变（4 model-mode configurations + 4 decoding strategies）\\n> - ⚠️ θ-sensitivity: θ=0.60 下 4/4 K*=2；θ=0.70 下仅 Config4-T12 K*=2（1v2=0.7039 仅超 \\\"suggestive\\\" 而非确定性结论。Config4-T12 正确 top-3 features: hapax_ratio, ttr, surp_skew（非 freq_entropy）\\n> - ⚠️ K*=2 边缘性双端注意: Qwen-cont 1v2 CI lower=0.6015（+0.15pp 高于 θ）+ Qwen-rw 2v3 CI upper=0.5991（-0.09pp 低于 θ），K*=2 边界在两端都极边缘。Discussion/Limitations 需同时提及\\n> - Paper 可写：\\\"K*=2 is established as a consistent discrimination boundary across all 8 tested conditions (4 model-mode configurations and 4 decoding strategies; bootstrap 95% CI for AUC₁ᵥ₂ > 0.60; AUC₂ᵥ₃ CI upper < 0.60), suggesting universality beyond the ≤1.5B scale studied.\\\"\\n> - ⚠️ 措辞策略（D017 Reviewer 评估）：分两层——\\\"established as consistent\\\" 用确定性语言（8/8 有统计检验支撑），\\\"suggesting universality beyond\\\" 对外推 hedging。不升级为 \\\"establishing universal\\\"——3 模型 ≤1.5B 会引发审稿人质疑消耗 rebuttal 预算\\n> - ⚠️ C1' 子组件定位：呈现为\\\"对同一 claim 的 4 个 evidence 维度\\\"而非 4 个独立 finding。§4 标题用 \\\"K* Characterization\\\"，每个子节开头显式回答一个 reviewer concern，子节间用 transitional sentences 链接\\n\\n**(b) Cross-model transfer**\\n> - Pythia↔OLMo 90-92% accuracy retention → K*=2 不是 model self-familiarity artifact\\n> - 0v1 AUC > 0.96 across all transfer pairs → binary contamination signal 完全跨模型可迁移\\n> - 1v2+ transfer 取决于模型族相似度 → 与 K*=2 完美一致（K* 之上分辨力本就微弱）\\n> - transfer retention 80-92% suggests depth signal has a large text-intrinsic component（不量化具体比例，因 transfer accuracy retention ≠ 信号分解）\\n> - ⚠️ 数值精度：Pythia→OLMo 91.6%, OLMo→Pythia 90.3%，范围 90-92%（非 91-92%）；Qwen-cont↔Pythia 84.7%/85.2%，范围 84-85%（非 83-85%）\\n\\n**(c) Degradation ordering consistency**\\n> - Spearman ρ ≥ 0.85 on single-feature AUC ranking（mode-controlled, continuation）\\n> - Top discriminative features shared: surp_d1_std, surp_std, surp_d2_std\\n> - Limitation: depth-pair level 一致性在 1v2 处较弱（τ~0.37）\\n> - Metric: single-feature AUC ranking（model-free, not LightGBM split count）\\n\\n**(e) Representation dependence of K* (§4.x Neural Probe / §5.x Discussion)**\\n> - exp-015: frozen Qwen-1.5B embeddings (1536-dim) + MLP probe → K*(neural)=3 on Qwen-cont（2v3 AUC=0.6195, fold range [0.6052, 0.6323]）\\n> - K* hierarchy: K*_theory ≥ K*_neural ≥ K*_15D，证明 K* 是表示复杂度的函数，K*_15D=2 是操作下界而非信息论极限\\n> - Interpretability–discriminability tradeoff: 15D features（K*=2, model-free, interpretable, deployable）vs neural probe（K*=3, model-dependent, opaque, 100x+ compute）\\n> - ⚠️ 当前仅 Qwen-cont 1 个数据集（Pythia/OLMo 待重跑）。若 Pythia/OLMo K*_neural=2 → self-familiarity 效应（Qwen embeddings 对 Qwen 生成文本更敏感），反而加强 15D model-free features 的定位\\n> - ⚠️ 2v3 边界极度边缘（fold 2 仅 +0.52pp 超 θ），需 bootstrap CI + permutation test 统计确认（重跑脚本已含）\\n> - 论文定位：**深化 K* characterization**（不推翻 K*=2 headline）。Headline 保持 \\\"K*_empirical=2 under 15D hand-crafted features\\\"，新增子结论 \\\"K*_neural=3 demonstrates representation-dependent boundary\\\"\\n> - Discussion 结构：§5.1 现有内容保留 + §5.1bis \\\"Representation Dependence of K*\\\" 独立子节（积极发现，非 limitation）\\n> - 预防审稿人 \\\"why bury the stronger result?\\\"：K*_15D=2 是 practical/deployable boundary，K*_neural=3 是 theoretical exploration，两者服务不同目的\\n\\n**(d) Generation mode analysis (§4.x Analysis subsection)**\\n> - Signal strength: rewrite AUC 1v2=0.6957 vs continuation 0.6114（Δ=+8.4pp）\\n> - Transferability: continuation 80-92% retention vs rewrite 61-63%\\n> - Feature subset: cont→rw 89% > rw→cont 82% → continuation features 是 rewrite 的通用子集\\n> - Interpretation: rewrite mode 的 instruction-following 引入 mode-specific style artifacts，放大 depth signal 但降低跨模型泛化性\\n> - 论文定位：Analysis subsection（\\\"How does generation mode affect depth estimation?\\\"），不是独立 claim\\n\\n### Secondary: C3 (Graduated Filtering)\\n即使在 K*=2 的粗粒度 ordinal signal（human/gen-1/gen-2+ 三级）下，K*-aware graduated filtering 仍优于 binary filtering，因为 ordinal signal 在阈值附近提供更 calibrated 的质量估计。\\n\\n### Supporting Analysis: Canonical Condition Selection\\nGeneration mode 是 depth estimation 的显著混淆变量。Methodology 段落阐述 continuation mode 作为 canonical condition 的选择及理由（不作为独立 claim）。\\n\\n## Story Spine\\n\\n- **Problem**: Web 数据正被多代递归合成文本污染。当前检测方法将合成文本视为 binary 类别（real vs synthetic），但质量随代际深度非线性退化——首代合成文本与四代合成文本的质量差异巨大却被忽视。\\n- **Existing approaches & gaps**: Binary detectors (DivEye, Min-K%++) 不区分代际；model collapse 文献 (Shumailov et al., Alemohammad et al. 2024, Dohmatob et al. 2024) 描述宏观现象但不提供检测工具；data selection methods (DSIR, D4, DoReMi) 做分布匹配但不估计递归深度；Drayson et al. (2025) 将 binary MGT detection 应用于 importance resampling 以防止 model collapse，是目前最接近的工作。然而这些方法均做二值判断，**不估计文本经历了多少轮递归生成，也不量化辨别能力的饱和边界。**\\n- **Our framing**: 将递归生成建模为 iterated lossy channel，从信息论（DPI）推导出 K* 的必然存在。K* 是首次系统测量的 operational quantity——它告诉数据策展者\\\"在什么深度以下，depth-aware curation 比 binary detection 更有价值\\\"。通过 8 条件实验矩阵（4 model-mode configurations + 4 decoding strategies）系统表征 K*，揭示其 universality：**K*=2 在所有测试条件下不变**，表明递归生成存在一个与具体模型架构和解码策略无关的 universal discrimination boundary。这一发现为数据治理提供 actionable guidance——depth-aware filtering 在 K* 以内有效，且无需针对不同模型或解码配置分别调优。\\n- **Headline result template**: \\\"Recursive generation has a universal discrimination boundary at K*=2: invariant across 4 model-mode configurations and 4 decoding strategies (8 conditions total) at ≤1.5B scale, with cross-model transfer retention of 80-92% confirming depth features are largely text-intrinsic. Even this coarse 3-class signal yields X% improvement over binary filtering on MMLU/HellaSwag.\\\"\\n\\n## Anticipated Reviewer Concerns & Responses\\n\\n1. **\\\"Universality claim 只基于 ≤1.5B 模型，能推广到更大模型吗？\\\"** → 这是最大 limitation，论文明确标注 \\\"at ≤1.5B scale\\\"；但 (i) universality 在 4 个不同架构族（GPT-2 style, Pythia, OLMo, Qwen）间已成立，说明不是单一架构的 artifact；(ii) DPI 理论保证 K*_theory 对任意规模模型存在，实证 K*_empirical 在更大模型上可能更大（信号更丰富）也可能不变（lossy channel 特性稳定），这是重要的 future work\\n2. **\\\"只是 feature engineering + off-the-shelf classifier，方法贡献弱\\\"** → 贡献在 problem formulation + empirical characterization（首次测量 K*）+ 理论框架（DPI）+ universality 发现，不是分类器本身\\n3. **\\\"Prompt-chain 不代表真实世界递归污染\\\"** → retrain-chain ecological validity check 作为补充；prompt-chain 是 controlled experiment 的标准方法\\n4. **\\\"K*=2 太浅，graduated filtering 收益有限\\\"** → universality 恰好说明这是 fundamental boundary 而非实验 artifact；即使 K*=2，3-class（human/gen-1/gen-2+）比 binary 在阈值附近更 calibrated（exp-006）；K* 的 universality 意味着实践者无需为每个模型/解码配置单独标定阈值\\n5. **\\\"同模型做生成和 surprisal 计算有 bias\\\"** → **exp-013 直接反驳**：Pythia↔OLMo 90-92% accuracy retention，0v1 transfer AUC > 0.96。Depth signal 主要编码在文本而非 model self-familiarity\\n6. **\\\"为什么不用 learned representations\\\"** → **exp-015 直接回应**：frozen LLM embeddings + MLP probe 达到 K*=3（vs 15D K*=2），揭示 representation-dependent hierarchy: K*_theory ≥ K*_neural ≥ K*_15D。Hand-crafted 15D features 的优势在于 model-free transferability（80-92% cross-model retention）、可解释性（支持 degradation ordering 分析）、实用可部署性。Neural representations 多辨别 1 个深度级别但代价是 model dependence + ~100x 计算开销。这一 tradeoff 证明 15D features 是 practical choice，同时表明 theoretical boundary 延伸至 K*=2 之上\\n7. **\\\"Graduated filtering 没和 data selection methods 比\\\"** → exp-006 包含 DSIR baseline + α sensitivity sweep\\n8. **\\\"Decoding strategy 只测了 4 种，coverage 不够\\\"** → 覆盖了实践中最常见的 decoding 配置（greedy, nucleus sampling, temperature scaling high/low）；4/4 完全一致强化 universality claim；extreme decoding（top-k=1 之外的 pathological 配置）留作 future work\\n9. **\\\"为什么不直接用 LLM 做 depth estimation\\\"** → **exp-011 直接反驳**：GPT-5.5 和 Claude Opus 4.6 在 500 样本 zero-shot 5-class depth 分类上仅 33.1-33.2% accuracy（MAE ~1.1），低于 OBD 15-feature LightGBM 的 38.6%。二者 binary detection 均 ~88%，说明 LLM 能区分 human vs synthetic 但无法估计递归深度。depth estimation 是非平凡任务，需要专用特征工程\\n\\n## Venue Fit\\n\\n- EMNLP 2026 special theme 明确呼吁 data contamination, synthetic data feedback loops, data governance 方向的工作\\n- 2+1 claim 结构：C1' 是 comprehensive characterization study with universality finding，C3 是 practical application，generation mode analysis 增加深度\\n- Universality narrative 比 controllability 更有 impact：从 \\\"K* depends on X, Y, Z\\\" 升级为 \\\"K*=2 is a universal boundary\\\"，审稿人更容易记住和传播\\n- 风险：universality claim 在 ≤1.5B 规模下的外推性是最大攻击面 → 论文中 scope 明确、limitation 诚实、DPI 理论支撑泛化的合理性\\n\", \"total_chars\": 10447, \"truncated\": false}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {}}"}
```

universality beyond\\\\\\\" 对外推 hedging。不升级为 \\\\\\\"establishing universal\\\\\\\"——3 模型 ≤1.5B 会引发审稿人质疑消耗 rebuttal 预算\\\\n> - ⚠️ C1' 子组件定位：呈现为\\\\\\\"对同一 claim 的 4 个 evidence 维度\\\\\\\"而非 4 个独立 finding。§4 标题用 \\\\\\\"K* Characterization\\\\\\\"，每个子节开头显式回答一个 reviewer concern，子节间用 transitional sentences 链接\\\\n\\\\n**(b) Cross-model transfer**\\\\n> - Pythia↔OLMo 90-92% accuracy retention → K*=2 不是 model self-familiarity artifact\\\\n> - 0v1 AUC > 0.96 across all transfer pairs → binary contamination signal 完全跨模型可迁移\\\\n> - 1v2+ transfer 取决于模型族相似度 → 与 K*=2 完美一致（K* 之上分辨力本就微弱）\\\\n> - transfer retention 80-92% suggests depth signal has a large text-intrinsic component（不量化具体比例，因 transfer accuracy retention ≠ 信号分解）\\\\n> - ⚠️ 数值精度：Pythia→OLMo 91.6%, OLMo→Pythia 90.3%，范围 90-92%（非 91-92%）；Qwen-cont↔Pythia 84.7%/85.2%，范围 84-85%（非 83-85%）\\\\n\\\\n**(c) Degradation ordering consistency**\\\\n> - Spearman ρ ≥ 0.85 on single-feature AUC ranking（mode-controlled, continuation）\\\\n> - Top discriminative features shared: surp_d1_std, surp_std, surp_d2_std\\\\n> - Limitation: depth-pair level 一致性在 1v2 处较弱（τ~0.37）\\\\n> - Metric: single-feature AUC ranking（model-free, not LightGBM split count）\\\\n\\\\n**(e) Representation dependence of K* (§4.x Neural Probe / §5.x Discussion)**\\\\n> - exp-015: frozen Qwen-1.5B embeddings (1536-dim) + MLP probe → K*(neural)=3 on Qwen-cont（2v3 AUC=0.6195, fold range [0.6052, 0.6323]）\\\\n> - K* hierarchy: K*_theory ≥ K*_neural ≥ K*_15D，证明 K* 是表示复杂度的函数，K*_15D=2 是操作下界而非信息论极限\\\\n> - Interpretability–discriminability tradeoff: 15D features（K*=2, model-free, interpretable, deployable）vs neural probe（K*=3, model-dependent, opaque, 100x+ compute）\\\\n> - ⚠️ 当前仅 Qwen-cont 1 个数据集（Pythia/OLMo 待重跑）。若 Pythia/OLMo K*_neural=2 → self-familiarity 效应（Qwen embeddings 对 Qwen 生成文本更敏感），反而加强 15D model-free features 的定位\\\\n> - ⚠️ 2v3 边界极度边缘（fold 2 仅 +0.52pp 超 θ），需 bootstrap CI + permutation test 统计确认（重跑脚本已含）\\\\n> - 论文定位：**深化 K* characterization**（不推翻 K*=2 headline）。Headline 保持 \\\\\\\"K*_empirical=2 under 15D hand-crafted features\\\\\\\"，新增子结论 \\\\\\\"K*_neural=3 demonstrates representation-dependent boundary\\\\\\\"\\\\n> - Discussion 结构：§5.1 现有内容保留 + §5.1bis \\\\\\\"Representation Dependence of K*\\\\\\\" 独立子节（积极发现，非 limitation）\\\\n> - 预防审稿人 \\\\\\\"why bury the stronger result?\\\\\\\"：K*_15D=2 是 practical/deployable boundary，K*_neural=3 是 theoretical exploration，两者服务不同目的\\\\n\\\\n**(d) Generation mode analysis (§4.x Analysis subsection)**\\\\n> - Signal strength: rewrite AUC 1v2=0.6957 vs continuation 0.6114（Δ=+8.4pp）\\\\n> - Transferability: continuation 80-92% retention vs rewrite 61-63%\\\\n> - Feature subset: cont→rw 89% > rw→cont 82% → continuation features 是 rewrite 的通用子集\\\\n> - Interpretation: rewrite mode 的 instruction-following 引入 mode-specific style artifacts，放大 depth signal 但降低跨模型泛化性\\\\n> - 论文定位：Analysis subsection（\\\\\\\"How does generation mode affect depth estimation?\\\\\\\"），不是独立 claim\\\\n\\\\n### Secondary: C3 (Graduated Filtering)\\\\n即使在 K*=2 的粗粒度 ordinal signal（human/gen-1/gen-2+ 三级）下，K*-aware graduated filtering 仍优于 binary filtering，因为 ordinal signal 在阈值附近提供更 calibrated 的质量估计。\\\\n\\\\n### Supporting Analysis: Canonical Condition Selection\\\\nGeneration mode 是 depth estimation 的显著混淆变量。Methodology 段落阐述 continuation mode 作为 canonical condition 的选择及理由（不作为独立 claim）。\\\\n\\\\n## Story Spine\\\\n\\\\n- **Problem**: Web 数据正被多代递归合成文本污染。当前检测方法将合成文本视为 binary 类别（real vs synthetic），但质量随代际深度非线性退化——首代合成文本与四代合成文本的质量差异巨大却被忽视。\\\\n- **Existing approaches & gaps**: Binary detectors (DivEye, Min-K%++) 不区分代际；model collapse 文献 (Shumailov et al., Alemohammad et al. 2024, Dohmatob et al. 2024) 描述宏观现象但不提供检测工具；data selection methods (DSIR, D4, DoReMi) 做分布匹配但不估计递归深度；Drayson et al. (2025) 将 binary MGT detection 应用于 importance resampling 以防止 model collapse，是目前最接近的工作。然而这些方法均做二值判断，**不估计文本经历了多少轮递归生成，也不量化辨别能力的饱和边界。**\\\\n- **Our framing**: 将递归生成建模为 iterated lossy channel，从信息论（DPI）推导出 K* 的必然存在。K* 是首次系统测量的 operational quantity——它告诉数据策展者\\\\\\\"在什么深度以下，depth-aware curation 比 binary detection 更有价值\\\\\\\"。通过 8 条件实验矩阵（4 model-mode configurations + 4 decoding strategies）系统表征 K*，揭示其 universality：**K*=2 在所有测试条件下不变**，表明递归生成存在一个与具体模型架构和解码策略无关的 universal discrimination boundary。这一发现为数据治理提供 actionable guidance——depth-aware filtering 在 K* 以内有效，且无需针对不同模型或解码配置分别调优。\\\\n- **Headline result template**: \\\\\\\"Recursive generation has a universal discrimination boundary at K*=2: invariant across 4 model-mode configurations and 4 decoding strategies (8 conditions total) at ≤1.5B scale, with cross-model transfer retention of 80-92% confirming depth features are largely text-intrinsic. Even this coarse 3-class signal yields X% improvement over binary filtering on MMLU/HellaSwag.\\\\\\\"\\\\n\\\\n## Anticipated Reviewer Concerns & Responses\\\\n\\\\n1. **\\\\\\\"Universality claim 只基于 ≤1.5B 模型，能推广到更大模型吗？\\\\\\\"** → 这是最大 limitation，论文明确标注 \\\\\\\"at ≤1.5B scale\\\\\\\"；但 (i) universality 在 4 个不同架构族（GPT-2 style, Pythia, OLMo, Qwen）间已成立，说明不是单一架构的 artifact；(ii) DPI 理论保证 K*_theory 对任意规模模型存在，实证 K*_empirical 在更大模型上可能更大（信号更丰富）也可能不变（lossy channel 特性稳定），这是重要的 future work\\\\n2. **\\\\\\\"只是 feature engineering + off-the-shelf classifier，方法贡献弱\\\\\\\"** → 贡献在 problem formulation + empirical characterization（首次测量 K*）+ 理论框架（DPI）+ universality 发现，不是分类器本身\\\\n3. **\\\\\\\"Prompt-chain 不代表真实世界递归污染\\\\\\\"** → retrain-chain ecological validity check 作为补充；prompt-chain 是 controlled experiment 的标准方法\\\\n4. **\\\\\\\"K*=2 太浅，graduated filtering 收益有限\\\\\\\"** → universality 恰好说明这是 fundamental boundary 而非实验 artifact；即使 K*=2，3-class（human/gen-1/gen-2+）比 binary 在阈值附近更 calibrated（exp-006）；K* 的 universality 意味着实践者无需为每个模型/解码配置单独标定阈值\\\\n5. **\\\\\\\"同模型做生成和 surprisal 计算有 bias\\\\\\\"** → **exp-013 直接反驳**：Pythia↔OLMo 90-92% accuracy retention，0v1 transfer AUC > 0.96。Depth signal 主要编码在文本而非 model self-familiarity\\\\n6. **\\\\\\\"为什么不用 learned representations\\\\\\\"** → **exp-015 直接回应**：frozen LLM embeddings + MLP probe 达到 K*=3（vs 15D K*=2），揭示 representation-dependent hierarchy: K*_theory ≥ K*_neural ≥ K*_15D。Hand-crafted 15D features 的优势在于 model-free transferability（80-92% cross-model retention）、可解释性（支持 degradation ordering 分析）、实用可部署性。Neural representations 多辨别 1 个深度级别但代价是 model dependence + ~100x 计算开销。这一 tradeoff 证明 15D features 是 practical choice，同时表明 theoretical boundary 延伸至 K*=2 之上\\\\n7. **\\\\\\\"Graduated filtering 没和 data selection methods 比\\\\\\\"** → exp-006 包含 DSIR baseline + α sensitivity sweep\\\\n8. **\\\\\\\"Decoding strategy 只测了 4 种，coverage 不够\\\\\\\"** → 覆盖了实践中最常见的 decoding 配置（greedy, nucleus sampling, temperature scaling high/low）；4/4 完全一致强化 universality claim；extreme decoding（top-k=1 之外的 pathological 配置）留作 future work\\\\n9. **\\\\\\\"为什么不直接用 LLM 做 depth estimation\\\\\\\"** → **exp-011 直接反驳**：GPT-5.5 和 Claude Opus 4.6 在 500 样本 zero-shot 5-class depth 分类上仅 33.1-33.2% accuracy（MAE ~1.1），低于 OBD 15-feature LightGBM 的 38.6%。二者 binary detection 均 ~88%，说明 LLM 能区分 human vs synthetic 但无法估计递归深度。depth estimation 是非平凡任务，需要专用特征工程\\\\n\\\\n## Venue Fit\\\\n\\\\n- EMNLP 2026 special theme 明确呼吁 data contamination, synthetic data feedback loops, data governance 方向的工作\\\\n- 2+1 claim 结构：C1' 是 comprehensive characterization study with universality finding，C3 是 practical application，generation mode analysis 增加深度\\\\n- Universality narrative 比 controllability 更有 impact：从 \\\\\\\"K* depends on X, Y, Z\\\\\\\" 升级为 \\\\\\\"K*=2 is a universal boundary\\\\\\\"，审稿人更容易记住和传播\\\\n- 风险：universality claim 在 ≤1.5B 规模下的外推性是最大攻击面 → 论文中 scope 明确、limitation 诚实、DPI 理论支撑泛化的合理性\\\\n\\\", \\\"total_chars\\\": 10447, \\\"truncated\\\": false}, \\\"error\\\": null, \\\"metadata\\\": {}}\"}",

| 61-63% retention | **upper bound with mode-specific confound** — 仅作 ablation |\\n\\n**原则**：\\n- C1' 的跨模型 claim 必须基于 continuation mode 数据\\n- Rewrite mode 的高 AUC (0.6957 vs cont 0.6114) 是 K*_empirical 在更大模型上可能更大（信号更丰富）也可能不变（lossy channel 特性稳定），这是重要的 future work\\n2. **\\\"只是 feature engineering + off-the-shelf classifier，方法贡献弱\\\"** → 贡献在 problem formulation + empirical characterization（首次测量 K*）+ 理论框架（DPI）+ universality 发现，不是分类器本身\\n3. **\\\"Prompt-chain 不代表真实世界递归污染\\\"** → retrain-chain ecological validity check 作为补充；prompt-chain 是 controlled experiment 的标准方法\\n4. **\\\"K*=2 太浅，graduated filtering 收益有限\\\"** → universality 恰好说明这是 fundamental boundary 而非实验 artifact；即使 K*=2，3-class（human/gen-1/gen-2+）比 binary 在阈值附近更 calibrated（exp-006）；K* 的 universality 意味着实践者无需为每个模型/解码配置单独标定阈值\\n5. **\\\"同模型做生成和 surprisal 计算有 bias\\\"** → **exp-013 直接反驳**：Pythia↔OLMo 90-92% accuracy retention，0v1 transfer AUC > 0.96。Depth signal 主要编码在文本而非 model self-familiarity\\n6. **\\\"为什么不用 learned representations\\\"** → **exp-015 直接回应**：frozen LLM embeddings + MLP probe 达到 K*=3（vs 15D K*=2），揭示 representation-dependent hierarchy: K*_theory ≥ K*_neural ≥ K*_15D。Hand-crafted 15D features 的优势在于 model-free transferability（80-92% cross-model retention）、可解释性（支持 degradation ordering 分析）、实用可部署性。Neural representations 多辨别 1 个深度级别但代价是 model dependence + ~100x 计算开销。这一 tradeoff 证明 15D features 是 practical choice，同时表明 theoretical boundary 延伸至 K*=2 之上\\n7. **\\\"Graduated filtering 没和 data selection methods 比\\\"** → exp-006 包含 DSIR baseline + α sensitivity sweep\\n8. **\\\"Decoding strategy 只测了 4 种，coverage 不够\\\"** → 覆盖了实践中最常见的 decoding 配置（greedy, nucleus sampling, temperature scaling high/low）；4/4 完全一致强化 universality claim；extreme decoding（top-k=1 之外的 pathological 配置）留作 future work\\n9. **\\\"为什么不直接用 LLM 做 depth estimation\\\"** → **exp-011 直接反驳**：GPT-5.5 和 Claude Opus 4.6 在 500 样本 zero-shot 5-class depth 分类上仅 33.1-33.2% accuracy（MAE ~1.1），低于 OBD 15-feature LightGBM 的 38.6%。二者 binary detection 均 ~88%，说明 LLM 能区分 human vs synthetic 但无法估计递归深度。depth estimation 是非平凡任务，需要专用特征工程\\n\\n## Venue Fit\\n\\n- EMNLP 2026 special theme 明确呼吁 data contamination, synthetic data feedback loops, data governance 方向的工作\\n- 2+1 claim 结构：C1' 是 comprehensive characterization study with universality finding，C3 是 practical application，generation mode analysis 增加深度\\n- Universality narrative 比 controllability 更有 impact：从 \\\"K* depends on X, Y, Z\\\" 升级为 \\\"K*=2 is a universal boundary\\\"，审稿人更容易记住和传播\\n- 风险：universality claim 在 ≤1.5B 规模下的外推性是最大攻击面 → 论文中 scope 明确、limitation 诚实、DPI 理论支撑泛化的合理性\\n\", \"total_chars\": 10447, \"truncated\": false}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {}}"
    }
  },

100	(Pythia vs.\ OLMo: $\Delta = 3.9$\,pp),
101	suggesting that generation protocol is a stronger determinant
102	of detectability than model identity.
103	We examine both factors in Sections~\ref{sec:transfer}
104	and~\ref{sec:generation_mode}.
105	
106	Beyond the $\Kempirical$ boundary, depth discrimination deteriorates
107	rapidly.
108	OLMo shows nonsignificant permutation $p$-values at 3v4
109	($p = 0.064$) and 4v5 ($p = 0.232$), indicating that depth
110	classification beyond generation~2 is indistinguishable from
111	random for this model.
112	Pythia retains marginal effects at these depths
113	($p = 0.006$ and $p = 0.008$, respectively),
114	while Qwen-continuation shows weak but detectable discrimination through
115	3v4 ($p = 0.002$) that fades at 4v5 ($p = 0.006$).
116	
117	%% ====================================================================
118	\subsection{Cross-Model Transfer}
119	\label{sec:transfer}
120	
121	Having established $\Kempirical = 2$ consistently, we next ask: is this boundary a property of the text itself, or an artifact of model self-familiarity?
122	To answer this, we train classifiers on one model's outputs and evaluate on another's.
123	Table~\ref{tab:transfer_matrix} shows the full
124	$4 \times 4$ transfer accuracy matrix for 6-class ordinal depth
125	classification.
126	
127	\begin{table}[t]
128	\centering
129	\small
130	\caption{Cross-model transfer accuracy (6-class ordinal
131	classification).  Diagonal entries (bold): within-model accuracy.
132	Off-diagonal: cross-model transfer.  Retention is computed as
133	off-diagonal accuracy divided by the diagonal entry of the training
134	model.}
135	\label{tab:transfer_matrix}
136	\begin{tabular}{lcccc}
137	\toprule
138	Train $\downarrow$ / Test $\rightarrow$
139	  & Qwen-rw & Pythia & OLMo & Qwen-cont \\
140	\midrule
141	Qwen-rw
142	  & \textbf{0.4229} & 0.2590 & 0.2653 & 0.3466 \\
143	Pythia
144	  & 0.2718 & \textbf{0.3856} & 0.3531 & 0.3287 \\
145	OLMo
146	  & 0.2768 & 0.3450 & \textbf{0.3822} & 0.3244 \\
147	Qwen-cont
148	  & 0.3440 & 0.3284 & 0.3112 & \textbf{0.3875} \\
149	\bottomrule
150	\end{tabular}
151	\end{table}
152	
153	\begin{figure}[t]
154	\centering
155	\includegraphics[width=0.85\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_transfer_heatmap.pdf}
156	\caption{Cross-model transfer heatmap (6-class ordinal accuracy).
157	Rows: training model; columns: test model.
158	Pythia$\leftrightarrow$OLMo (both continuation mode) shows high
159	mutual transfer (90--92\% accuracy retention), while Qwen-rewrite
160	transfers poorly to continuation-mode models (61--63\%).
161	Diagonal entries are within-model baselines.}
162	\label{fig:transfer_heatmap}
163	\end{figure}
164	
165	Two principal findings emerge (Figure~\ref{fig:transfer_heatmap}).
166	First, transfer between continuation-mode models is high.
167	Pythia$\leftrightarrow$OLMo mutual transfer retains 90--92\% of within-model accuracy
168	(85--86\% retention by Kendall $\tau$).
169	The accuracy-based retention of 90--92\% is complemented by the
170	$\tau$-based retention of 85--86\%, indicating that not only
171	classification accuracy but also the relative depth ordering of
172	predictions is largely preserved.
173	Qwen-continuation$\leftrightarrow$Pythia also transfers well
174	(84--85\% accuracy retention),
175	and Qwen-continuation$\leftrightarrow$OLMo achieves 80--85\%.
176	These retention rates---80--92\% of within-model accuracy preserved across continuation-mode pairs---suggest that a large component of the depth signal is text-intrinsic rather than tied to a specific generator.
177	
178	Second, Qwen-rewrite exhibits catastrophic transfer failure.
179	Classifiers trained on Qwen-rewrite retain only 61--63\% of
180	within-model accuracy when evaluated on Pythia or OLMo
181	(45--50\% by $\tau$).
182	The reverse direction transfers asymmetrically: Pythia/OLMo${\to}$Qwen-rewrite retains 70--72\% of within-model accuracy, higher than the forward 61--63\% but still far below continuation-mode transfer rates (80--92\%).
183	This confirms that rewrite-mode classifiers rely on generation
184	artifacts that do not generalize across models, in contrast
185	to Qwen-continuation, which achieves 80--85\% retention to the same targets.
186	
187	At the 0v1 boundary, cross-model pairwise $\auc$ exceeds 0.96
188	for all model pairs, confirming that the human-vs.-synthetic
189	distinction is consistently transferable across all tested generators
190	and modes.
191	The transfer degradation concentrates at deeper boundaries
192	(1v2 and beyond), where model- and mode-specific factors dominate.
193	
194	The aggregate picture is one of modest cross-model loss:
195	mean pairwise $\auc$ is 0.6099 within-model versus 0.5905
196	cross-model.
197	This gap is driven primarily by Qwen-rewrite; among
198	continuation-mode pairs the within/cross difference narrows
199	further.
200	
201	%% ====================================================================
202	\subsection{Feature Degradation Ordering}
203	\label{sec:feature_degradation}
204	
205	The transfer results indicate that depth signals are largely text-intrinsic.
206	We next ask what drives discrimination: if the signal is text-intrinsic, different generators should rely on the same features in the same order.
207	We test this by computing single-feature $\auc$ rankings
208	independently for each model and comparing them via rank
209	correlation.
210	
211	\begin{table}[t]
212	\centering
213	\small
214	\caption{Top-3 features by single-feature $\auc$ ranking for each
215	model configuration.
216	Features shared across all three continuation-mode models are in bold.
217	Qwen-rewrite's top feature (\texttt{freq\_entropy}) is mode-specific.
218	Spearman $\rho$ (continuation-mode pairs): Pythia--OLMo 0.95,
219	Pythia--Qwen-continuation 0.9143, OLMo--Qwen-continuation 0.85.}
220	\label{tab:top_features}
221	\begin{tabular}{cllll}
222	\toprule
223	Rank & Pythia & OLMo & Qwen-rw & Qwen-cont \\
224	\midrule
225	1
226	  & \textbf{\texttt{surp\_d1\_std}}
227	  & \textbf{\texttt{surp\_std}}
228	  & \texttt{freq\_entropy}
229	  & \textbf{\texttt{surp\_d1\_std}} \\
230	2
231	  & \textbf{\texttt{surp\_d2\_std}}
232	  & \textbf{\texttt{surp\_d1\_std}}
233	  & \textbf{\texttt{surp\_std}}
234	  & \textbf{\texttt{surp\_std}} \\
235	3
236	  & \textbf{\texttt{surp\_std}}
237	  & \textbf{\texttt{surp\_d2\_std}}
238	  & \textbf{\texttt{surp\_d1\_std}}
239	  & \textbf{\texttt{surp\_d2\_std}} \\
240	\bottomrule
241	\end{tabular}
242	\end{table}
243	
244	Among continuation-mode models, single-feature $\auc$ rankings
245	are highly correlated:
246	Pythia vs.\ OLMo $\rho = 0.95$,
247	Pythia vs.\ Qwen-continuation $\rho = 0.9143$,
248	OLMo vs.\ Qwen-continuation $\rho = 0.85$
249	(all $\rho \geq 0.85$).
250	The top-3 features---\texttt{surp\_d1\_std}, \texttt{surp\_std},
251	and \texttt{surp\_d2\_std}---are shared (up to ordering) across
252	all three continuation-mode configurations
253	(Table~\ref{tab:top_features}).
254	All three measure variability of surprisal or its derivatives,
255	capturing the \emph{heterogeneity} of token-level predictability
256	across the text.
257	That these features degrade in a shared order across generators
258	suggests a common mechanism: each generation step smooths the
259	token-level surprisal landscape, reducing its variance in a
260	model-independent fashion.
261	
262	Qwen-rewrite diverges: its top-ranked feature is
263	\texttt{freq\_entropy}, a lexical diversity measure absent from
264	the continuation-mode top-3 for any model.
265	This corroborates the transfer results in
266	Section~\ref{sec:transfer}---rewrite-mode classifiers learn
267	partly from instruction-following artifacts rather than the
268	shared surprisal-based signals.
269	
270	Kendall $\tau$ provides a complementary, more conservative
271	perspective:
272	Pythia vs.\ OLMo $\tau = 0.733$,
273	Pythia vs.\ Qwen-continuation $\tau = 0.695$,
274	OLMo vs.\ Qwen-continuation $\tau = 0.543$.
275	Cross-mode correlations are lower:
276	Pythia vs.\ Qwen-rewrite $\tau = 0.467$,
277	OLMo vs.\ Qwen-rewrite $\tau = 0.429$.
278	
279	\paragraph{Limitation.}
280	The high Spearman $\rho$ characterizes the \emph{aggregate}
281	feature ranking, but at the depth-pair level the agreement
282	weakens: $\tau \approx 0.37$ at the 1v2 boundary.
283	This means that while the overall importance ordering is shared,
284	the relative contribution of individual features at the
285	discrimination boundary---where precision matters most---varies
286	across models.
287	We view this as a genuine limitation of the text-intrinsic
288	interpretation and a direction for future investigation.
289	
290	%% ====================================================================
291	\subsection{Generation Mode Effect}
292	\label{sec:generation_mode}
293	
294	Finally, we ask whether generation mode---an experimental design choice rather than a text property---affects depth detectability.
295	Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct provides a controlled comparison: rewrite (instruction-based paraphrasing) and continuation (prompt truncation) share the same model weights, as defined in Section~\ref{sec:method}, isolating the effect of generation protocol.
296	
297	Rewrite mode yields higher 1v2 $\auc$ than continuation:
298	0.6955 vs.\ 0.6112, a difference of $+$8.4 percentage points.
299	The advantage persists at 2v3
300	(0.5893 vs.\ 0.5609, $\Delta = +$2.8\,pp)
301	and narrows at 3v4
302	(0.5435 vs.\ 0.5409, $\Delta = +$0.3\,pp),
303	with both modes converging toward chance at deeper levels.
304	The amplified detectability under rewrite mode is consistent
305	with instruction-following introducing systematic deviations
306	from the source text's style---additional artifacts that are
307	discriminable but mode-specific rather than text-intrinsic.
308	
309	Transfer between modes is asymmetric.
310	A classifier trained on Qwen-continuation and evaluated on Qwen-rewrite
311	retains 89\% of within-model accuracy
312	(0.3440 vs.\ 0.3875 diagonal),
313	whereas the reverse direction---Qwen-rewrite trained,
314	Qwen-continuation tested---retains 82\%
315	(0.3466 vs.\ 0.4229 diagonal).
316	The direction of the asymmetry is intuitive:
317	continuation-mode signals form a subset of the information
318	available in rewrite-mode data
319	(which adds instruction-following artifacts on top),
320	so a continuation-trained classifier can partially recognize
321	rewrite-mode outputs but not vice versa.
322	
323	This asymmetry is mirrored in the feature rankings.
324	Qwen-rewrite's top feature (\texttt{freq\_entropy}) is
325	mode-specific and ranks outside the top-3 for every
326	continuation-mode model.
327	Qwen-continuation's top feature (\texttt{surp\_d1\_std}) is shared
328	with Pythia and OLMo, confirming that continuation mode
329	captures primarily text-intrinsic depth signals.
330	
331	The practical implication is a detectability--generalizability
332	tradeoff.
333	Rewrite-mode classifiers achieve higher absolute $\auc$ but
334	transfer poorly across models
335	(Qwen-rewrite$\to$Pythia/OLMo: 61--63\% retention).
336	Continuation-mode classifiers sacrifice 8.4\,pp of within-model
337	$\auc$ but achieve robust cross-model transfer
338	(Qwen-continuation$\to$Pythia: 84--85\%).
339	For deployment scenarios requiring model-agnostic depth
340	estimation, continuation-mode features provide a more reliable
341	foundation despite lower raw discriminability.
342	
```

### Tool result

_source line 475 · 2026-05-14T08:39:45.400Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01C2Gvd4kdhTPkJitLCQEGMR`</summary>

```text
24	Dohmatob et al.~\cite{dohmatob2024model} derived scaling laws demonstrating that tail probability mass is lost at rates predictable from model and data scale.
25	Our $\Kempirical = 2$ provides a complementary, classifier-based measurement of this phenomenon: the information-theoretic discrimination boundary is reached after just two generation steps.
26	Alemohammad et al.~\cite{alemohammad2024selfconsuming} proved convergence to a fixed point for self-consuming generative models; $\Kempirical = 2$ indicates that, operationally, the approach to this fixed point is rapid enough that adjacent-generation distinctions collapse within two steps.
27	
28	Importantly, $\Kempirical = 2$ is an \emph{operational} lower bound conditioned on the 15-dimensional hand-crafted feature set; the \emph{theoretical} boundary $\Ktheory$ may be strictly higher.
29	Preliminary evidence from a neural probing experiment supports this distinction.
30	A frozen Qwen2.5-1.5B encoder (1536-dimensional last-layer representations) paired with a lightweight MLP classifier (Table~\ref{tab:s7_neural_probe}) achieves 2v3 $\auc = 0.6195$ ($\text{std} = 0.0094$) on the Qwen-continuation dataset, exceeding the $\theta = 0.60$ threshold that the same dataset's LightGBM classifier fails to cross ($\auc = 0.5609$; Section~\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).
31	This yields $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}} = 3$: the learned representation recovers one additional discriminable boundary that the hand-crafted features miss.
32	The improvement is concentrated at the 2v3 pair ($+$5.86\,pp over LightGBM); at the already-separable 1v2 boundary the neural probe achieves 0.6983 versus 0.6112, a substantial gain but one that does not alter the boundary determination.
33	At 3v4, the neural probe reaches $\auc = 0.5930$---below $\theta$ but closer to the threshold than any hand-crafted configuration---suggesting that a still richer representation might push the boundary further.
34	The lossy-channel narrative remains intact: even with 1536-dimensional frozen representations, the discrimination boundary extends to only three steps, confirming that the information bottleneck of recursive generation is shallow even with substantially richer representations.
35	These results are preliminary: they cover a single model-dataset pair (Qwen-continuation) and lack bootstrap confidence intervals; cross-model validation on Pythia and OLMo is pending.
36	
37	The consistency of $\Kempirical = 2$ across decoding strategies merits emphasis.
38	Greedy decoding, nucleus sampling ($p{=}0.9$), $T{=}0.7$, and $T{=}1.2$ all yield $\Kempirical = 2$ (Figure~\ref{fig:auc_decay}; Table~\ref{tab:s2_decoding}), with 1v2 $\auc$ spanning a narrow range of [0.6866, 0.7039].
39	Higher decoding entropy modestly increases signal strength ($\auc_{1,2}$: 0.69 greedy $\to$ 0.70 at $T{=}1.2$) but does not shift the boundary.
40	This robustness suggests that, at least at the $\leq$1.5B scale, $\Kempirical$ is governed primarily by the information bottleneck of the generation process rather than by surface-level properties of the sampling algorithm.
41	
42	\subsection{Implications of Cross-Model Transfer}
43	\label{sec:discuss_transfer}
44	
45	The cross-model transfer results (Section~\ref{sec:transfer}) indicate that depth signals are largely encoded in text-level statistical properties rather than generator-specific artifacts.
46	Classifiers trained on Pythia-1.4B and evaluated on OLMo-1B retain 90--92\% of within-model accuracy, and the broader continuation-mode transfer matrix shows 80--92\% retention across all model pairs (Table~\ref{tab:transfer_matrix}; see Table~\ref{tab:s1_transfer} for the full matrix).
47	At the 0v1 boundary, cross-model $\auc$ exceeds 0.96 across all tested model pairs, confirming that binary contamination detection is fully transferable across the tested generators.
48	
49	The practical implication is direct: a single depth classifier trained on one model's outputs can be deployed to estimate generational depth for texts produced by other models, without model-specific retraining.
50	This transferability extends the utility of depth estimation to open-web settings where the generating model is unknown---precisely the scenario where depth-aware data curation is most needed.
51	
52	Two caveats bound this conclusion.
53	First, the text-intrinsic component dominates, as evidenced by high cross-model transfer retention (80--92\% across continuation-mode pairs), yet a nontrivial model-specific residual persists.
54	The Pythia$\leftrightarrow$OLMo pair, which spans different architectures and training corpora, achieves the highest cross-family retention (90--92\%), consistent with the depth signal being largely architecture-agnostic within continuation mode.
55	Second, Qwen-continuation$\leftrightarrow$OLMo retention (80--85\%) is lower than Pythia$\leftrightarrow$OLMo (90--92\%), indicating that model family similarity modulates transfer even among continuation-mode configurations.
56	
57	\subsection{Feature Importance Across Decoding Strategies}
58	\label{sec:discuss_features}
59	
60	While $\Kempirical = 2$ is consistent across decoding strategies (4/4 configurations), the feature importance ordering is not equally stable.
61	Greedy decoding and $T{=}0.7$ share highly correlated feature rankings (Spearman $\rho = 0.914$), with top features dominated by vocabulary diversity measures: \texttt{self\_bleu}, \texttt{hapax\_ratio}, and \texttt{ttr}.
62	In contrast, greedy versus $T{=}1.2$ yields a lower correlation ($\rho = 0.53$, $p = 0.041$; suggestive but nonsignificant after Bonferroni correction for 6 pairwise comparisons), with $T{=}1.2$ promoting \texttt{hapax\_ratio}, \texttt{ttr}, and \texttt{surp\_skew} to the top ranks.
63	Notably, \texttt{freq\_entropy} rises from rank~10 under greedy ($\auc = 0.5139$) to rank~5 under $T{=}1.2$ ($\auc = 0.5905$), illustrating how high-entropy decoding amplifies frequency-distribution artifacts.
64	
65	This dissociation reveals an important distinction: the \emph{existence} of the depth boundary is robust, but the \emph{optimal feature subset} for detecting it depends on the sampling strategy.
66	High-entropy decoding amplifies frequency-distribution artifacts while attenuating vocabulary-diversity signals, shifting which text properties carry the most discriminative power without altering whether adjacent generations can be distinguished.
67	For practitioners, this implies that a general-purpose depth classifier should incorporate features from multiple groups---surprisal moments, vocabulary diversity, and frequency tail statistics---to maintain performance across unknown decoding conditions.
68	
69	The cross-model feature ordering (Section~\ref{sec:feature_degradation}) provides a complementary perspective: among continuation-mode models, single-feature $\auc$ rankings are highly consistent (Spearman $\rho \geq 0.85$), with surprisal-variance features---\texttt{surp\_d1\_std}, \texttt{surp\_std}, \texttt{surp\_d2\_std}---dominating across all three families.
70	A genuine limitation, however, is that depth-pair-level agreement weakens to $\tau \approx 0.37$ at the 1v2 boundary (Section~\ref{sec:feature_degradation}), indicating that the relative contribution of individual features at the discrimination boundary varies across models even when the aggregate ordering is shared.
71	
72	\subsection{Scale Considerations}
73	\label{sec:discuss_scale}
```

```text
88	
89	\subsection{LLM Zero-Shot Baseline}
90	\label{sec:discuss_llm}
91	
92	Frontier LLMs provide a natural baseline for depth estimation.
93	On a 500-sample test set with 5 depth classes (depths 0--4), excluding depth~5 from the 6-class range used in the main feature-based experiments since depths $\geq 3$ are mutually indistinguishable ($\Kempirical = 2$), GPT-5.5 achieves 33.2\% accuracy (MAE = 1.10) and Claude Opus~4.6 achieves 33.1\% accuracy (MAE = 1.15)---both below the OBD-LightGBM baseline of 38.6\% (MAE = 1.11; see Table~\ref{tab:s3_llm_zeroshot} for per-depth confusion matrices).
94	Simultaneously, both models achieve approximately 88\% binary accuracy on the human-vs.-synthetic distinction (GPT-5.5: 87.8\%, Claude: 88.2\%).
95	
96	This gap between binary detection and depth estimation is informative.
97	Frontier LLMs can reliably determine \emph{whether} a text is machine-generated but cannot estimate \emph{how many generations of recursive processing} it has undergone.
98	Depth estimation requires sensitivity to subtle distributional shifts between adjacent generations---shifts orders of magnitude smaller than the human-vs.-machine gap.
99	The 15-dimensional statistical feature pipeline, despite its simplicity, captures these fine-grained shifts more effectively than frontier LLMs in a zero-shot setting.
100	This motivates the dedicated feature-engineering approach: the task demands specialized statistical sensitivity rather than general language understanding.
101	We evaluate only zero-shot prompting; few-shot or chain-of-thought strategies may narrow the gap and warrant future investigation.
102	
103	\subsection{Limitations}
104	\label{sec:limitations}
105	
106	We identify seven limitations that bound the generalizability of our findings.
107	
108	\emph{Model scale.}
109	All experiments use models at or below 1.5B parameters (Pythia-1.4B, OLMo-1B, Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct).
110	Larger models may produce higher-fidelity text with weaker depth signatures, potentially shifting $\Kempirical$ beyond~2.
111	
112	\emph{Prompt-chain versus retrain-chain.}
113	Our recursive generation protocol is a prompt-chain: the same fixed model repeatedly generates from the previous output.
114	Real-world model collapse involves retrain-chains, where models are retrained on data from earlier generations~\cite{shumailov2024collapse}.
115	Retrain-chains introduce additional degradation through training dynamics, potentially producing different $\Kstar$ values.
116	
117	\emph{Language and domain.}
118	All experiments use English text from the C4 corpus.
119	Depth dynamics may differ across languages, domains (e.g., code, scientific text), and source corpora with different distributional properties.
120	
121	\emph{Decoding coverage.}
122	We test four decoding strategies (greedy, nucleus $p{=}0.9$, $T{=}0.7$, $T{=}1.2$).
123	Other strategies---top-$k$ sampling, beam search, contrastive decoding---remain untested and may yield different feature importance patterns or boundary behavior.
124	
125	\emph{Threshold sensitivity.}
126	$\Kempirical$ depends on the operational threshold $\theta$.
127	$\Kempirical \geq 2$ across $\theta \in \{0.55, 0.60, 0.65\}$ for all four decoding strategies, rising to $\Kempirical = 3$ at $\theta = 0.55$ (all four 2v3 $\auc$ values exceed 0.55) and returning to $\Kempirical = 2$ at $\theta \in \{0.60, 0.65\}$.
128	At $\theta = 0.70$, three of four decoding configurations collapse to $\Kempirical = 1$; only the high-temperature setting ($T{=}1.2$) retains $\Kempirical = 2$, consistent with its elevated 1v2 $\auc$ (0.7039).
129	Beyond decoding parameters, the threshold sensitivity extends to generation mode: at $\theta{=}0.70$, $\Kempirical{=}1$ for all four model-mode configurations; at $\theta{=}0.65$, continuation-mode configurations already drop to $\Kempirical{=}1$, while rewrite mode retains $\Kempirical{=}2$.
130	Our current analysis primarily covers the decoding configuration axis.
131	The boundary is robust within a practical range but not invariant to threshold choice; our conclusions are conditioned on $\theta = 0.60$.
132	
133	\emph{Boundary marginality.}
134	The $\Kempirical = 2$ determination is statistically significant but numerically marginal at both ends.
135	On the lower side, Qwen-continuation's 1v2 CI lower bound (0.6015) exceeds $\theta = 0.60$ by only $+$0.15\,pp.
136	On the upper side, Qwen-rewrite's 2v3 CI upper bound (0.5991) falls below $\theta$ by only $-$0.09\,pp.
137	This narrow margin means that small changes in the feature set or classifier could shift the boundary---as the neural probe results confirm, where a richer representation pushes the 2v3 pair above $\theta$ on the Qwen-continuation dataset (Section~\ref{sec:discuss_kstar}).
```

中显式提及 random baseline performance（一句话即可），避免审稿人手动计算后质疑 \"38.6% 并不高\"。\n- OBD accuracy 38.6% vs random 16.7% = +21.9pp，vs LLM 33% = +5.6pp。这个对比应该更 explicit。\n\n**(c) [severity: info] Feature ablation {"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\"\\\"\\\"exp006 Analysis — aggregate results, paired t-tests, alpha sensitivity.\\n\\nInput:  outputs/exp006/*/eval_results.json\\nOutput: summary JSON + formatted tables to stdout\\n\\nDependencies: numpy, scipy, json, glob\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nimport argparse\\nimport json\\nimport os\\nimport re\\nfrom collections import defaultdict\\nfrom glob import glob\\n\\nimport numpy as np\\nfrom scipy import stats\\n\\n\\ndef parse_tag(dirname):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Extract (strategy, alpha, seed) from directory name like 'graduated_a0.5_s42'.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    base = os.path.basename(dirname)\\n    m_grad = re.match(r\\\"(graduated)_a([\\\\d.]+)_s(\\\\d+)\\\", base)\\n    if m_grad:\\n        return m_grad.group(1), float(m_grad.group(2)), int(m_grad.group(3))\\n    m_other = re.match(r\\\"(.+)_s(\\\\d+)\\\", base)\\n    if m_other:\\n        return m_other.group(1), None, int(m_other.group(2))\\n    return None, None, None\\n\\n\\ndef extract_scores(result):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Pull MMLU acc and HellaSwag acc_norm from lm-eval result JSON.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    scores = result.get(\\\"scores\\\", {})\\n    mmlu = None\\n    hellaswag = None\\n\\n    for task_name, metrics in scores.items():\\n        if \\\"mmlu\\\" in task_name.lower():\\n            mmlu = metrics.get(\\\"acc,none\\\", metrics.get(\\\"acc_norm,none\\\"))\\n        if \\\"hellaswag\\\" in task_name.lower():\\n            hellaswag = metrics.get(\\\"acc_norm,none\\\", metrics.get(\\\"acc,none\\\"))\\n\\n    return mmlu, hellaswag\\n\\n\\ndef paired_ttest(scores_a, scores_b, seeds_a, seeds_b):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Paired t-test on matched seeds. Returns (t_stat, p_value, n_pairs).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    common = sorted(set(seeds_a) & set(seeds_b))\\n    if len(common) < 2:\\n        return None, None, len(common)\\n    a = [scores_a[seeds_a.index(s)] for s in common]\\n    b = [scores_b[seeds_b.index(s)] for s in common]\\n    t, p = stats.ttest_rel(a, b)\\n    return float(t), float(p), len(common)\\n\\n\\ndef main():\\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=\\\"exp006 analysis\\\")\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--results-dir\\\", type=str, default=\\\"outputs/exp006\\\")\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--output\\\", type=str, default=None,\\n                        help=\\\"Save summary JSON\\\")\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--best-alpha-metric\\\", type=str, default=\\\"mmlu\\\",\\n                        choices=[\\\"mmlu\\\", \\\"hellaswag\\\", \\\"avg\\\"])\\n    args = parser.parse_args()\\n\\n    # --- Collect results ---\\n    result_files = sorted(glob(os.path.join(args.results_dir, \\\"*/eval_results.json\\\")))\\n    if not result_files:\\n        print(f\\\"No results found in {args.results_dir}\\\")\\n        return\\n\\n    # strategy -> {seeds: [], mmlu: [], hellaswag: []}\\n    data = defaultdict(lambda: {\\\"seeds\\\": [], \\\"mmlu\\\": [], \\\"hellaswag\\\": []})\\n    # graduated alpha sweep: (alpha,) -> same structure\\n    alpha_data = defaultdict(lambda: {\\\"seeds\\\": [], \\\"mmlu\\\": [], \\\"hellaswag\\\": []})\\n\\n    # Load base model results (no adapter, no training)\\n    base_result_path = os.path.join(args.results_dir, \\\"base_model\\\", \\\"eval_results.json\\\")\\n    if os.path.exists(base_result_path):\\n        with open(base_result_path) as f:\\n            base_result = json.load(f)\\n        base_mmlu, base_hs = extract_scores(base_result)\\n        if base_mmlu is not None or base_hs is not None:\\n            data[\\\"base (no training)\\\"] = {\\n                \\\"seeds\\\": [0], \\\"mmlu\\\": [base_mmlu], \\\"hellaswag\\\": [base_hs]\\n            }\\n\\n    for fpath in result_files:\\n        dirname = os.path.dirname(fpath)\\n        strategy, alpha, seed = parse_tag(dirname)\\n        if strategy is None:\\n            continue\\n\\n        with open(fpath) as f:\\n            result = json.load(f)\\n        mmlu, hellaswag = extract_scores(result)\\n\\n        if mmlu is None and hellaswag is None:\\n            print(f\\\"  WARN: no scores in {fpath}\\\")\\n            continue\\n\\n        if strategy == \\\"graduated\\\" and alpha is not None:\\n            alpha_data[alpha][\\\"seeds\\\"].append(seed)\\n            alpha_data[alpha][\\\"mmlu\\\"].append(mmlu)\\n            alpha_data[alpha][\\\"hellaswag\\\"].append(hellaswag)\\n        else:\\n            data[strategy][\\\"seeds\\\"].append(seed)\\n            data[strategy][\\\"mmlu\\\"].append(mmlu)\\n            data[strategy][\\\"hellaswag\\\"].append(hellaswag)\\n\\n    # For graduated, pick bes\\n\\n... --- vs base one-sample t-tests ---\\n    base_d = data.get(\\\"base (no training)\\\")\\n    if base_d and base_d[\\\"mmlu\\\"][0] is not None:\\n        print()\\n        print(\\\"One-sample t-tests vs base model\\\")\\n        print(\\\"-\\\" * 80)\\n        base_mmlu_val = base_d[\\\"mmlu\\\"][0]\\n        base_hs_val = base_d[\\\"hellaswag\\\"][0]\\n        for sa in [\\\"no-filter\\\", \\\"binary-ours\\\", \\\"binary-drayson\\\",\\n                    \\\"graduated\\\", \\\"surprisal\\\", \\\"dsir\\\"]:\\n            da = data.get(sa)\\n            if not da or len(da[\\\"mmlu\\\"]) < 2:\\n                continue\\n            for metric, base_val in [(\\\"mmlu\\\", base_mmlu_val), (\\\"hellaswag\\\", base_hs_val)]:\\n                if base_val is None:\\n                    continue\\n                vals = da[metric]\\n                t_stat, p_val = stats.ttest_1samp(vals, base_val)\\n                sig = \\\"\\\"\\n                if p_val < 0.01:\\n                    sig = \\\" **\\\"\\n                elif p_val < 0.05:\\n                    sig = \\\" *\\\"\\n                diff = np.mean(vals) - base_val\\n                print(f\\\"  {sa} vs base ({metric}): diff={diff:+.4f}, \\\"\\n                      f\\\"t={t_stat:.3f}, p={p_val:.4f}, n={len(vals)}{sig}\\\")\\n                ttests[f\\\"{sa}_vs_base_{metric}\\\"] = {\\n                    \\\"t\\\": float(t_stat), \\\"p\\\": float(p_val), \\\"n\\\": len(vals)\\n                }\\n\\n    # {"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\"\\\"\\\"exp006 Evaluation — lm-eval-harness (MMLU + HellaSwag) on LoRA-adapted model.\\n\\nInput:  base model name + LoRA adapter path (or --base-only for unadapted model)\\nOutput: JSON with task scores in --output path\\n\\nDependencies: lm_eval>=0.4, torch, transformers, peft\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nimport argparse\\nimport json\\nimport os\\nimport torch\\n\\n\\ndef run_eval(model_name, adapter_path, tasks, batch_size, cache_dir, output_path):\\n    import lm_eval\\n\\n    model_args = f\\\"pretrained={model_name},dtype=bfloat16,trust_remote_code=True\\\"\\n    if cache_dir:\\n        model_args += f\\\",cache_dir={cache_dir}\\\"\\n    if adapter_path:\\n        model_args += f\\\",peft={adapter_path}\\\"\\n\\n    print(f\\\"Evaluating: {model_args}\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"Tasks: {tasks}\\\")\\n\\n    results = lm_eval.simple_evaluate(\\n        model=\\\"hf\\\",\\n        model_args=model_args,\\n        tasks=tasks,\\n        batch_size=batch_size,\\n        device=\\\"cuda:0\\\",\\n    )\\n\\n    # Extract scores\\n    scores = {}\\n    for task_name, task_results in results[\\\"results\\\"].items():\\n        task_scores = {}\\n        for metric, value in task_results.items():\\n            if isinstance(value, (int, float)):\\n                task_scores[metric] = value\\n        scores[task_name] = task_scores\\n\\n    output = {\\n        \\\"model\\\": model_name,\\n        \\\"adapter\\\": adapter_path,\\n        \\\"tasks\\\": tasks,\\n        \\\"scores\\\": scores,\\n    }\\n\\n    if output_path:\\n        os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(output_path), exist_ok=True)\\n        with open(output_path, \\\"w\\\") as f:\\n            json.dump(output, f, indent=2)\\n        print(f\\\"Results saved to {output_path}\\\")\\n\\n    # Print summary\\n    for task, s in scores.items():\\n        main_metric = s.get(\\\"acc_norm,none\\\", s.get(\\\"acc,none\\\", \\\"N/A\\\"))\\n        print(f\\\"  {task}: {main_metric}\\\")\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.7}}"}

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"#!/bin/bash\\n# exp006 orchestrator — run all strategy x seed combinations (train + eval).\\n#\\n# Usage:\\n#   bash run_exp006.sh --strategy all --seeds \\\"42 123 456 789 1024\\\"\\n#   bash run_exp006.sh --strategy graduated --alpha \\\"0.1 0.3 0.5 0.7 0.9\\\"\\n#   bash run_exp006.sh --strategy binary-ours --seeds \\\"42\\\"\\nset -euo pipefail\\n\\nPROJECT_DIR=\\\"/root/autodl-tmp/gen-depth-contamination\\\"\\nOUTPUT_BASE=\\\"${PROJECT_DIR}/outputs/exp006\\\"\\nSCRIPTS_DIR=\\\"${PROJECT_DIR}/scripts\\\"\\nDATA_DIR=\\\"${PROJECT_DIR}/data\\\"\\nMODEL=\\\"/root/autodl-tmp/.hf_cache/Qwen/Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct/Qwen/Qwen2___5-1___5B-Instruct\\\"\\nCACHE_DIR=\\\"/root/autodl-tmp/.hf_cache\\\"\\n\\nALL_STRATEGIES=\\\"no-filter binary-ours binary-drayson graduated surprisal dsir\\\"\\nSTRATEGIES=\\\"$ALL_STRATEGIES\\\"\\nSEEDS=\\\"42 123 456 789 1024\\\"\\nALPHAS=\\\"0.1 0.3 0.5 0.7 0.9\\\"\\n\\nwhile [[ $# -gt 0 ]]; do\\n    case $1 in\\n        --strategy)\\n            if [ \\\"$2\\\" = \\\"all\\\" ]; then STRATEGIES=\\\"$ALL_STRATEGIES\\\"; else STRATEGIES=\\\"$2\\\"; fi\\n            shift 2 ;;\\n        --seeds)  SEEDS=\\\"$2\\\";  shift 2 ;;\\n        --alpha)  ALPHAS=\\\"$2\\\"; shift 2 ;;\\n        *) echo \\\"Unknown: $1\\\"; exit 1 ;;\\n    esac\\ndone\\n\\nsource /root/miniconda3/etc/profile.d/conda.sh\\nsource /etc/network_turbo 2>/dev/null\\nexport HF_HOME=/root/autodl-tmp/.hf_cache\\nexport HF_HUB_DISABLE_XET=1\\nexport REQUESTS_CA_BUNDLE=/etc/ssl/certs/ca-certificates.crt\\nexport SSL_CERT_FILE=/etc/ssl/certs/ca-certificates.crt\\n# HF_ENDPOINT not needed — model loaded from local path, datasets via network_turbo proxy\\nconda activate base\\n\\nmkdir -p \\\"$OUTPUT_BASE\\\"\\n\\n# Base model baseline (no adapter, no training)\\nBASE_RESULT=\\\"${OUTPUT_BASE}/base_model/eval_results.json\\\"\\nif [ ! -f \\\"$BASE_RESULT\\\" ]; then\\n    echo \\\"=== Evaluating base model (no adapter) ===\\\"\\n    mkdir -p \\\"${OUTPUT_BASE}/base_model\\\"\\n    python3 \\\"${SCRIPTS_DIR}/exp006_evaluate.py\\\" \\\\\\n        --model-name \\\"$MODEL\\\" \\\\\\n        --output \\\"$BASE_RESULT\\\" \\\\\\n        --cache-dir \\\"$CACHE_DIR\\\"\\nfi\\n\\n# Count runs\\ntotal_runs=0\\nfor s in $STRATEGIES; do\\n    for seed in $SEEDS; do\\n        if [ \\\"$s\\\" = \\\"graduated\\\" ]; then\\n            for _ in $ALPHAS; do total_runs=$((total_runs + 1)); done\\n        else\\n            total_runs=$((total_runs + 1))\\n        fi\\n    done\\ndone\\n\\n# ~25 min per run (15 min train + 10 min eval on 4090 with 1.5B LoRA)\\nest_hours=$(echo \\\"scale=1; $total_runs * 25 / 60\\\" | bc)\\necho \\\"=== exp006 Graduated Filtering Evaluation ===\\\"\\necho \\\"Strategies: $STRATEGIES\\\"\\necho \\\"Seeds:      $SEEDS\\\"\\necho \\\"Alphas:     $ALPHAS (graduated only)\\\"\\necho \\\"Total runs: $total_runs\\\"\\necho \\\"Est. GPU time: ~${est_hours}h (~${total_runs}x25min)\\\"\\necho \\\"\\\"\\n\\nrun_one() {\\n    local strategy=$1 seed=$2 alpha=$3 tag=$4\\n    local out_dir=\\\"${OUTPUT_BASE}/${tag}\\\"\\n    local result_file=\\\"${out_dir}/eval_results.json\\\"\\n\\n    if [ -f \\\"$result_file\\\" ]; then\\n        echo \\\"  SKIP (results exist)\\\"\\n        return 0\\n    fi\\n\\n    local extra_args=\\\"\\\"\\n    if [ \\\"$strategy\\\" = \\\"graduated\\\" ]; then\\n        extra_args=\\\"--alpha $alpha\\\"\\n    fi\\n\\n    echo \\\"  Training...\\\"\\n    python3 \\\"${SCRIPTS_DIR}/exp006_continued_pretraining.py\\\" \\\\\\n        --model-name \\\"$MODEL\\\" --data-dir \\\"$DATA_DIR\\\" \\\\\\n        --output-dir \\\"$out_dir\\\" --strategy \\\"$strategy\\\" \\\\\\n        --seed \\\"$seed\\\" --cache-dir \\\"$CACHE_DIR\\\" $extra_args\\n\\n    echo \\\"  Evaluating...\\\"\\n    python3 \\\"${SCRIPTS_DIR}/exp006_evaluate.py\\\" \\\\\\n        --model-name \\\"$MODEL\\\" \\\\\\n        --adapter-path \\\"${out_dir}/final_adapter\\\" \\\\\\n        --output \\\"$result_file\\\" --cache-dir \\\"$CACHE_DIR\\\"\\n\\n    sleep 3\\n}\\n\\nidx=0\\nfailed=0\\nfor strategy in $STRATEGIES; do\\n    if [ \\\"$strategy\\\" = \\\"graduated\\\" ]; then\\n        for alpha in $ALPHAS; do\\n            for seed in $SEEDS; do\\n                idx=$((idx + 1))\\n                tag=\\\"${strategy}_a${alpha}_s${seed}\\\"\\n                echo \\\"[$idx/$total_runs] $tag\\\"\\n                if ! run_one \\\"$strategy\\\" \\\"$seed\\\" \\\"0\\\" \\\"$tag\\\"; then\\n                echo \\\"FAILED: $tag\\\" | tee -a \\\"${OUTPUT_BASE}/failures.log\\\"\\n                failed=$((failed + 1))\\n            fi\\n        done\\n    fi\\ndone\\n\\necho \\\"\\\"\\necho \\\"=== Complete: $((total_runs - failed))/$total_runs succeeded ===\\\"\\nif [ $failed -gt 0 ]; then\\n    echo \\\"Failures logged in ${OUTPUT_BASE}/failures.log\\\"\\nfi\\necho \\\"Run analysis: python3 ${SCRIPTS_DIR}/exp006_analyze.py --results-dir ${OUTPUT_BASE}\\\"\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.7}}"}
